  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kali adalah dewi Hindu kematian, waktu, dan akhir zaman. Ia sering dikaitkan dengan seksualitas dan kekerasan, tetapi juga dipandang sebagai sosok ibu yang kuat dan lambang kasih keibuan. Kali mewujudkan shakti, energi feminin, kreativitas, dan kesuburan, dan merupakan inkarnasi Parvati, istri Shiva. Ia terutama dipuja di Bengal sebagai dewi utama aliran sakta, disebut juga Kalika, dan dianggap yang paling terkenal di antara banyak dewi Hindu. Ia juga yang pertama dari sepuluh Mahavidya, dewi pemberi pengetahuan yang membebaskan, dan dewa terkemuka dalam tradisi tantrik Hindu.",
      "en": "Kali is the Hindu goddess of death, time, and doomsday. Though often linked with sexuality and violence, she is also regarded as a powerful mother figure and a symbol of a mother's love. Kali embodies shakti, the energy of the feminine, of creativity and of fertility, and is regarded as an incarnation of Parvati, Shiva's wife. She is particularly worshipped in Bengal as the premier goddess of the sakta cult, is also called Kalika, and is held to be the most famous of the many Hindu goddesses. She is also the first of the ten Mahavidyas, goddesses who provide liberating knowledge, and the preeminent deity in Hindu tantric traditions.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kali-c01",
        "kali-c02",
        "kali-c10",
        "kali-c03",
        "kali-c47",
        "kali-c56",
        "kali-c48",
        "kali-c58"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kali diceritakan lahir dalam beberapa versi. Dalam Devi Mahatmya, teks sekitar abad ke-6 M, ia muncul dari dahi atau amarah Durga atau Kaushiki saat sang dewi bertempur melawan iblis seperti Chanda dan Munda, lalu menerima nama Chamunda. Dalam versi lain ia lahir ketika Parvati melepas kulit gelapnya, atau ketika Parvati bersatu dengan racun di kerongkongan Shiva untuk membunuh Daruka, atau dari gabungan shakti para dewa untuk menghancurkan Raktabija.",
      "en": "Kali's birth is told in several versions. In the Devi Mahatmya, a text from around the sixth century CE, she emerges from the brow or anger of Durga or Kaushiki as the goddess fights demons such as Chanda and Munda, and then receives the name Chamunda. In other versions she is born when Parvati sheds her dark skin, or when Parvati combines with the poison in Shiva's throat to kill Daruka, or from the combined shakti of the gods to destroy Raktabija.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kali-c30",
        "kali-c05",
        "kali-c52",
        "kali-c31",
        "kali-c17",
        "kali-c18",
        "kali-c19"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah Kali yang paling terkenal adalah kematian Raktabija, iblis yang melahirkan iblis baru dari tiap tetes darahnya yang jatuh ke tanah. Kali memenggal kepalanya dengan pedang dan meminum seluruh darahnya agar tak ada lagi yang jatuh ke tanah. Dalam kisah lain, setelah lahir Kali mengamuk dan memakan semua iblis yang ditemuinya; Shiva menghentikannya dengan berbaring di jalannya, dan ketika Kali menyadari siapa yang diinjaknya, ia tenang. Dari kisah itu dijelaskan kaitan Kali dengan medan pertempuran dan tempat kremasi.",
      "en": "Kali's most famous story is the death of Raktabija, the demon from whose every drop of blood that touched the ground a new demon arose. Kali lopped off his head with a sword and drank all his blood so that none fell to the ground. In another story, once born Kali went wild and ate every demon she met; Shiva stopped her by lying down in her path, and when Kali realised who she was standing on, she calmed down. That story explains Kali's association with battlegrounds and cremation grounds.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kali-c06",
        "kali-c14",
        "kali-c15",
        "kali-c16"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam seni, Kali paling sering digambarkan sebagai sosok petarung yang menakutkan, berkulit biru atau hitam, dengan kalung kepala, rok lengan, lidah terjulur, dan pisau yang meneteskan darah. Ia berlengan banyak dan memegang pedang, trisula, cawan, dan senjata lain; pose paling umum menampilkannya berdiri atau menari dengan satu kaki di atas Shiva yang roboh. Di Tamil Nadu dan Kerala ia juga digambarkan dalam wujud yang menyenangkan sebagai dewi ibu, dan di Bengal sejak Abad Pertengahan ia dipuja sebagai Kali Ma, ibu yang baik hati.",
      "en": "In art Kali is most often shown as a fearsome fighting figure with blue or black skin, a necklace of heads, a skirt of arms, a lolling tongue, and a knife dripping with blood. She has many arms holding a sword, trident, cup, and other weapons; the most common pose shows her standing or dancing with one foot on a collapsed Shiva. In Tamil Nadu and Kerala she also appears in a \"pleasing\" form as a kindly mother goddess, and in Bengal since the medieval period she has been worshipped as Kali Ma, the benevolent mother.",
      "claim_ids": [

  "cultural_context": {
    "id": "Sebuah himne Tantrik, Karpuradistotra, menggambarkan Kali sebagai sumber kekayaan dan kesuburan serta penolakan duniawi dan kematian. Menurut Encyclopedia.com, Kali mempersonifikasikan ambivalensi dewa dalam tradisi Hindu, dalam siklus kehidupan dan kematian: penciptaan, penghancuran, dan pembaruan. Kali Puja, perayaan besar bagi sang dewi sejak abad ketujuh belas, jatuh pada malam bulan baru bulan Kartik dan sering bertepatan dengan Diwali. Gerakan nasionalis Bengal juga memakainya sebagai simbol India, dan di masa modern ia menjadi simbol penting bagi perempuan.",
    "en": "A Tantric hymn, the Karpuradistotra, describes Kali as the source of wealth and fertility but also of renunciation and death. According to Encyclopedia.com, Kali personifies the ambivalence of deity in the Hindu tradition, in the cycle of life and death: creation, destruction, and regeneration. Kali Puja, a major festival for the goddess since the 17th century, falls on the new moon night of Kartik and often coincides with Diwali. The Bengali nationalist movement also used her as a symbol of India, and in modern times she has become a significant symbol for women.",

      "summary": {
        "id": "Dalam Devimahatmya, Kali muncul dari dahi atau amarah Durga (dalam versi Wikipedia, Kaushiki) saat sang dewi menyerang iblis Chanda dan Munda; Kali langsung mengalahkan keduanya dan menerima nama Chamunda. Versi lain menyebut amarah Durga menyembur dari dahinya sebagai Kali.",

    },
    {
      "title": {
        "id": "Kali dan para pencuri",
        "en": "Kali and the thieves"
      },
      "role": {
        "id": "Dewi yang membela biksu",
        "en": "The goddess who defends the monk"
      },
      "summary": {
        "id": "Sekawanan pencuri hendak mempersembahkan korban manusia, seorang biksu Brahmana, di depan patung Kali ketika patung itu tiba-tiba hidup. Murka, sang dewi memenggal seluruh gerombolan itu sementara sang Brahmana melarikan diri.",
        "en": "A band of thieves prepared to sacrifice a Brahmin monk in front of the statue of Kali when the statue suddenly came to life. Outraged, the goddess decapitated the whole gang while the Brahmin escaped."
      },
      "claim_ids": [
        "kali-c21",
        "kali-c22"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "tradition_vs_modern": {
    "traditional": {
      "id": "Menurut Encyclopedia.com, bagi sebagian besar pemuja masa kini Kali tidak dipandang murka, dan di Bengal sejak Abad Pertengahan ia dipuja sebagai Kali Ma, ibu yang baik hati.",
      "en": "According to Encyclopedia.com, for most modern worshippers Kali is not seen as wrathful, and in Bengal since the medieval period she has been worshipped as Kali Ma, the benevolent mother."
    },
    "modern": {
      "id": "Di Barat, non-Hindu dikenalkan pada Kali sebagai dewi jahat dalam beberapa film abad ke-20, termasuk Gunga Din, Help!, dan Indiana Jones and the Temple of Doom.",
      "en": "In the West, non-Hindus were introduced to Kali as a villainous goddess in several twentieth-century films, including Gunga Din, Help!, and Indiana Jones and the Temple of Doom."
    },
    "claim_ids": [
      "kali-c44",
      "kali-c40",
      "kali-c43"
    ]
  },
  "learning_questions": [
    {
      "id": "Mengapa dewi yang digambarkan menakutkan juga dipuja sebagai ibu yang melindungi?",
      "en": "Why is a goddess shown as fearsome also worshipped as a protective mother?"
    },
    {
      "id": "Apa yang berbeda antara berbagai kisah kelahiran Kali, dan apa artinya bagi cara orang memahaminya?",
      "en": "How do the various stories of Kali's birth differ, and what do they mean for how people understand her?"
    },
    {
      "id": "Bagaimana penggambaran Kali dalam film berbeda dari pemahaman para pemujanya?",
      "en": "How does the portrayal of Kali in films differ from how her worshippers understand her?"
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kali-c01",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "Kali is the Hindu goddess of death, time, and doomsday.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kali adalah dewi kematian, waktu, dan akhir zaman dalam Hindu.",
        "en": "Kali is the Hindu goddess of death, time, and doomsday."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c02",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "She is often associated with sexuality and violence but is also considered a strong mother figure and symbol of motherly love.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kali juga dipandang sebagai sosok ibu dan lambang kasih keibuan.",
        "en": "Kali is also seen as a mother figure and symbol of motherly love."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c03",
      "source_id": "kali-s2",
      "quote": "Kali, the supreme embodiment of female power (shakti), is a manifestation of the goddess Parvati, Shiva’s loving wife, and is particularly worshipped in Bengal as the premier goddess of the sakta cult.",
      "locator": "Object description",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kali dipahami sebagai perwujudan Parvati dan terutama dipuja di Bengal.",
        "en": "Kali is understood as a manifestation of Parvati and is particularly worshipped in Bengal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c04",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "Kali's name derives from the Sanskrit meaning 'she who is black' or 'she who is death', but she is also known as Chaturbhuja Kali, Chinnamastā, or Kaushika.",
      "locator": "Name & Worship",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Kali dikaitkan dengan makna Sansekerta 'yang hitam' atau 'yang mati'.",
        "en": "Kali's name is linked to Sanskrit expressions for blackness or death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c05",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "Durga became so enraged that her anger burst from her forehead in the form of Kali.",
      "locator": "Kali's Birth",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu kisah menyebut Kali muncul dari kemarahan Durga.",
        "en": "One story says Kali emerges from Durga's anger."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c06",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "Raktabija himself was killed when Kali lopped off his head with a sword and then drank all of his blood, making sure none fell to the ground and thereby ensuring no more demons could menace the world.",
      "locator": "Kali & Raktabija",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kali membunuh Raktabija dan meminum darahnya agar tidak jatuh ke tanah.",
        "en": "Kali kills Raktabija and drinks his blood so none reaches the ground."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c07",
      "source_id": "kali-s2",
      "quote": "She is typically represented standing on the prostrate figure of her husband, Shiva, who here is seen lying in a charnel ground, his trident and drum abandoned.",
      "locator": "Object description",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebuah gambar menampilkan Kali berdiri di atas Shiva yang terbaring.",
        "en": "An artwork depicts Kali standing above the prostrate Shiva."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c08",
      "source_id": "kali-s3",
      "quote": "Among the earliest textual references to Kali is the sixth-century devotional text Devi Mahatmya (“Glory of the Goddess”), where she emerges as the personified wrath or the embodied fury of the goddess Durga.",
      "locator": "Textual references",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu rujukan awal tentang Kali ada dalam Devi Mahatmya abad keenam.",
        "en": "One of the earliest references to Kali appears in the sixth-century Devi Mahatmya."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c09",
      "source_id": "kali-s3",
      "quote": "Since the seventeenth century, Kali Puja (or Shyama Puja), has been a major Hindu festival dedicated to the goddess, celebrated on the new moon night (Amavasya) of the month of Kartik, often coinciding with Diwali.",
      "locator": "Kali Puja",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kali Puja adalah perayaan untuk Kali pada malam bulan baru bulan Kartik.",
        "en": "Kali Puja honors Kali on the new moon of Kartik."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c10",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "Kali embodies shakti - feminine energy, creativity and fertility - and is an incarnation of Parvati, wife of the great Hindu god Shiva.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kali mewujudkan shakti, yaitu energi feminin, kreativitas, dan kesuburan, dan merupakan inkarnasi Parvati, istri dewa Hindu Shiva.",
        "en": "Kali embodies shakti, feminine energy, creativity and fertility, and is an incarnation of Parvati, wife of the Hindu god Shiva."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c11",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "Kali is most often represented in art as a fearful fighting figure with a necklace of heads, skirt of arms, lolling tongue, and brandishing a knife dripping with blood.",
      "locator": "Introduction",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam seni, Kali paling sering digambarkan sebagai sosok petarung yang menakutkan dengan kalung kepala, rok lengan, lidah terjulur, dan pisau yang meneteskan darah.",
        "en": "In art Kali is most often shown as a fearsome fighting figure with a necklace of heads, a skirt of arms, a lolling tongue, and a knife dripping with blood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c12",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "As an embodiment of time Kali devours all things, she is irresistibly attractive to mortals and gods, and can also represent (particularly in later traditions) the benevolence of a mother goddess.",
      "locator": "Name & Worship",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai perwujudan waktu, Kali melahap segala sesuatu, memikat manusia dan dewa tak tertahankan, dan (terutama dalam tradisi kemudian) juga dapat melambangkan kebaikan dewi ibu.",
        "en": "As an embodiment of time Kali devours all things, is irresistibly attractive to mortals and gods, and can also represent, particularly in later traditions, the benevolence of a mother goddess."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c13",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "The goddess (devi) is particularly worshipped in eastern and southern India and specifically in Assam, Kerala, Kashmir, Bengal - where she is now worshipped in the yearly festival of Kali Puja held on the night of a new moon - and in the Kalighat Temple in the city of Calcutta.",
      "locator": "Name & Worship",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kali terutama dipuja di India timur dan selatan, khususnya di Assam, Kerala, Kashmir, Bengal (dengan perayaan tahunan Kali Puja pada malam bulan baru), dan di Kuil Kalighat di kota Calcutta.",
        "en": "Kali is particularly worshipped in eastern and southern India, especially in Assam, Kerala, Kashmir, Bengal (with the yearly Kali Puja festival on the night of a new moon), and at the Kalighat Temple in Calcutta."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c14",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "Once born, the black goddess went wild and ate all the demons she came across, stringing their heads on a chain which she wore around her neck.",
      "locator": "Kali's Birth",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah lahir, sang dewi hitam mengamuk dan memakan semua iblis yang ditemuinya, merangkai kepala mereka menjadi rantai di lehernya.",
        "en": "Once born, the black goddess went wild and ate all the demons she came across, stringing their heads on a chain around her neck."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c15",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "Fortunately, the mighty Shiva stopped Kali's destructive rampage by lying down in her path, and when the goddess realised just who she was standing on, she finally calmed down.",
      "locator": "Kali's Birth",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shiva menghentikan amukan Kali dengan berbaring di jalannya, dan ketika Kali menyadari siapa yang diinjaknya, ia akhirnya tenang.",
        "en": "Shiva stopped Kali's destructive rampage by lying down in her path, and when she realised who she was standing on, she finally calmed down."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c16",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "From this story is explained Kali's association with battlegrounds and areas where cremation is carried out.",
      "locator": "Kali's Birth",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dari kisah ini dijelaskan kaitan Kali dengan medan pertempuran dan tempat kremasi.",
        "en": "This story explains Kali's association with battlegrounds and areas where cremation is carried out."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c17",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "In another version of the goddess' birth, Kali appeared when Parvati shed her dark skin which then became Kali, hence one of her names is Kaushika (the Sheath), whilst Parvati is left as Gauri (the Fair One).",
      "locator": "Kali's Birth",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam versi lain, Kali muncul ketika Parvati melepas kulit gelapnya yang lalu menjadi Kali, sehingga salah satu namanya Kaushika (Sarung), sedangkan Parvati tetap sebagai Gauri (Si Cantik).",
        "en": "In another version Kali appeared when Parvati shed her dark skin, which became Kali, hence one of her names is Kaushika (the Sheath), while Parvati remains Gauri (the Fair One)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c18",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "By combining with the poison still held in Shiva's throat, Parvati was transformed into Kali. Leaping from Shiva's throat in her new guise, Kali swiftly despatched Daruka and all was well with the world once more.",
      "locator": "Kali's Birth",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam versi ketiga, Parvati berubah menjadi Kali dengan bersatu dengan racun di kerongkongan Shiva, lalu melompat keluar dan dengan cepat membunuh Daruka.",
        "en": "In a third version Parvati became Kali by combining with the poison in Shiva's throat, then leapt out and swiftly despatched Daruka."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c19",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "The gods decided to work together and combine all of their shakti or divine energy and produce one super being that could destroy Raktabija; the result was Kali (in another version only Durga produces Kali).",
      "locator": "Kali & Raktabija",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para dewa menggabungkan seluruh shakti mereka untuk menciptakan satu makhluk yang bisa menghancurkan Raktabija, dan hasilnya adalah Kali (dalam versi lain hanya Durga yang menciptakan Kali).",
        "en": "The gods combined all their shakti to produce one super being that could destroy Raktabija, and the result was Kali (in another version only Durga produces Kali)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c20",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "Given all the divine weapons of the gods, Kali swiftly sought out Raktabija and his demons and proceeded to swallow them all whole so as not to spill any more blood in the process.",
      "locator": "Kali & Raktabija",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dengan semua senjata ilahi para dewa, Kali mencari Raktabija dan iblis-iblisnya dan menelan mereka bulat-bulat agar tidak ada lagi darah yang tumpah.",
        "en": "Given all the gods' divine weapons, Kali sought out Raktabija and his demons and swallowed them whole so that no more blood would be spilled."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c21",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "The thieves wanted to make a human sacrifice to Kali, and unwisely chose a Brahmin monk as a likely victim. Dragging him to the nearest temple, the thieves prepared to make the sacrifice in front of the statue of Kali when suddenly the statue came to life.",
      "locator": "Kali & Raktabija",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah lain, sekawanan pencuri hendak mempersembahkan korban manusia, seorang biksu Brahmana, kepada Kali, tetapi patung Kali tiba-tiba hidup.",
        "en": "In another story a band of thieves prepared to make a human sacrifice of a Brahmin monk to Kali, when suddenly the statue of Kali came to life."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c22",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "Outraged at the thieves' plan to kill a monk, the goddess took swift revenge and decapitated the whole gang, even tossing their heads about for fun, whilst naturally the Brahmin escaped",
      "locator": "Kali & Raktabija",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Murka atas rencana itu, sang dewi membalas dengan memenggal seluruh gerombolan itu, sementara sang Brahmana melarikan diri.",
        "en": "Outraged at the plan, the goddess took swift revenge and decapitated the whole gang, while the Brahmin escaped."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c23",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "In art, Kali in her feminine form is most often portrayed with blue or black skin, naked, and wearing a Bengali type crown of clay which is painted or gilded.",
      "locator": "Kali In Hindu Art",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam seni, Kali dalam wujud perempuannya paling sering digambarkan berkulit biru atau hitam, telanjang, dan bermahkota tanah liat ala Bengal yang dicat atau disepuh.",
        "en": "In art Kali in her feminine form is most often shown with blue or black skin, naked, and wearing a Bengali-type clay crown, painted or gilded."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c24",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "She is, like many Hindu deities, a multiple-armed figure with the number of arms being four, eight, ten, twelve, or even eighteen. Each arm usually holds an object and these can include a sword, dagger, trident, cup, drum, chakra, lotus bud, whip, noose, bell, and shield.",
      "locator": "Kali In Hindu Art",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kali digambarkan berlengan banyak, empat hingga delapan belas, dan tiap tangan biasanya memegang benda seperti pedang, belati, trisula, cawan, genderang, cakra, kuncup teratai, cambuk, jerat, lonceng, dan perisai.",
        "en": "Kali is shown with many arms, from four to eighteen, each usually holding an object such as a sword, dagger, trident, cup, drum, chakra, lotus bud, whip, noose, bell, or shield."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c25",
      "source_id": "kali-s1",
      "quote": "Kali's most common pose in paintings is in her most fearsome guise as the slayer of demons, where she stands or dances with one foot on a collapsed Shiva and holds a severed head.",
      "locator": "Kali In Hindu Art",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pose Kali yang paling umum dalam lukisan adalah sebagai pembunuh iblis, berdiri atau menari dengan satu kaki di atas Shiva yang roboh sambil memegang kepala terpenggal.",
        "en": "Kali's most common pose in paintings is as slayer of demons, standing or dancing with one foot on a collapsed Shiva and holding a severed head."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c26",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "Traditionally, Kali was portrayed in both literature and iconography in her \"terrifying\" or \"fierce\" (ugra) aspect, but in Tamil Nadu and Kerala she has also been depicted in a \"pleasing\" (saumya) form, as a benevolent mother goddess.",
      "locator": "Kali",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Secara tradisional Kali digambarkan dalam sastra dan ikonografi dengan sisi \"menakutkan\" atau \"garang\" (ugra), tetapi di Tamil Nadu dan Kerala ia juga digambarkan dalam wujud \"menyenangkan\" (saumya) sebagai dewi ibu yang baik hati.",
        "en": "Traditionally Kali was portrayed in literature and iconography in her \"terrifying\" or \"fierce\" (ugra) aspect, but in Tamil Nadu and Kerala she has also been depicted in a \"pleasing\" (saumya) form as a benevolent mother goddess."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c27",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "She thus personifies the ambivalence of deity in the Hindu tradition, which manifests itself in the unceasing cycle of life and death-creation, destruction, and regeneration.",
      "locator": "Kali",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Encyclopedia.com, Kali dengan demikian mempersonifikasikan ambivalensi dewa dalam tradisi Hindu, yang tampak dalam siklus tak berkesudahan kehidupan dan kematian: penciptaan, penghancuran, dan pembaruan.",
        "en": "According to Encyclopedia.com, Kali personifies the ambivalence of deity in the Hindu tradition, which shows in the unceasing cycle of life and death: creation, destruction, and regeneration."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c28",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "Kali probably derives from a local village goddess (gramadevata), who became integrated into the Hindu pantheon through identification with an aspect of Parvati, the consort of Shiva.",
      "locator": "Kali",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Encyclopedia.com, Kali kemungkinan berasal dari dewi desa setempat (gramadevata) yang masuk ke jajaran dewa Hindu lewat identifikasi dengan salah satu aspek Parvati, permaisuri Shiva.",
        "en": "According to Encyclopedia.com, Kali probably derives from a local village goddess (gramadevata) who was integrated into the Hindu pantheon through identification with an aspect of Parvati, consort of Shiva."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c29",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "Kali is first mentioned as a distinct, but peripheral and probably non-Aryan, goddess in the Mahabharata.",
      "locator": "Kali in the Hindu Texts",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kali pertama kali disebut sebagai dewi yang khas, tetapi pinggiran dan kemungkinan non-Arya, dalam Mahabharata.",
        "en": "Kali is first mentioned as a distinct but peripheral and probably non-Aryan goddess in the Mahabharata."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c30",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "In the section of this text known as the Devimahatmya, composed around the sixth century ce, Kali is said to have emanated from the brow of the goddess Durga during one of her battles with demonic forces.",
      "locator": "Kali in the Hindu Texts",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam bagian Devimahatmya (disusun sekitar abad ke-6 M), Kali dikatakan memancar dari dahi dewi Durga saat salah satu pertempurannya melawan pasukan iblis.",
        "en": "In the Devimahatmya (composed around the sixth century CE), Kali is said to have emanated from the brow of the goddess Durga during one of her battles with demonic forces."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c31",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "In the Devimahatmya, Kali is generated from the anger of Durga, as the goddess attacks the demons Chanda and Munda. After this event, Kali receives the compound name Chamunda.",
      "locator": "Kali in the Hindu Texts",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Devimahatmya, Kali lahir dari amarah Durga saat sang dewi menyerang iblis Chanda dan Munda; setelah itu Kali menerima nama majemuk Chamunda.",
        "en": "In the Devimahatmya Kali is generated from Durga's anger as the goddess attacks the demons Chanda and Munda; afterwards Kali receives the compound name Chamunda."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c32",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "Kali cut the demon in two with her sword, but from every drop of blood that fell to the ground there sprang a new demon. So she consumed the duplicated demons, drank the drops before they touch the ground, and sucked the lifeblood from Raktabija.",
      "locator": "Kali in the Hindu Texts",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kali membelah iblis itu dengan pedang, tetapi dari tiap tetes darah yang jatuh muncul iblis baru; maka ia melahap iblis-iblis tiruan itu, meminum tetesan sebelum menyentuh tanah, dan menghisap darah kehidupan Raktabija.",
        "en": "Kali cut the demon in two with her sword, but each drop of blood that fell produced a new demon; so she consumed the duplicates, drank the drops before they touched the ground, and sucked the lifeblood from Raktabija."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c33",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "after her victory, Kali became so intoxicated by the destruction of battle that her rampage threatened to destroy the whole world until Shiva manifested himself as a baby, crying in the middle of the corpse-strewn field. Kali calmed down as she suckled the baby.",
      "locator": "Kali in the Hindu Texts",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Encyclopedia.com, dalam kisah Linga Purana, setelah menang Kali begitu mabuk oleh kehancuran sehingga amukannya mengancam dunia, sampai Shiva menjelma bayi yang menangis di medan penuh mayat dan Kali tenang ketika menyusui bayi itu.",
        "en": "According to Encyclopedia.com, in the Linga Purana account, after her victory Kali became so intoxicated by destruction that her rampage threatened the world until Shiva appeared as a crying baby in the corpse-strewn field and Kali calmed as she suckled the baby."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c34",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "Kali is also the feminine form of the word kala-time; as such, she is the representation of time, which is all-destroying and all-devouring.",
      "locator": "Kali in the Hindu Texts",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kali juga bentuk feminin kata kala (waktu); dengan demikian ia mewakili waktu, yang menghancurkan dan melahap segalanya.",
        "en": "Kali is also the feminine form of the word kala, time; as such she represents time, which is all-destroying and all-devouring."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c35",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "Indeed, the name Kali is related to a term first used in the Mundaka Upanishad (1.2.4) for one of the seven flickering tongues of the sacrificial fire, Agni.",
      "locator": "Kali in the Hindu Texts",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Kali berkaitan dengan istilah yang pertama kali dipakai dalam Mundaka Upanishad (1.2.4) untuk salah satu dari tujuh lidah api kurban, Agni.",
        "en": "The name Kali is related to a term first used in the Mundaka Upanishad (1.2.4) for one of the seven flickering tongues of the sacrificial fire, Agni."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c36",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "A Tantric hymn to Kali, the Karpuradistotra, describes her as the source of not only wealth and fertility but also renunciation and death.",
      "locator": "Kali in the Hindu Texts",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebuah himne Tantrik untuk Kali, Karpuradistotra, menggambarkannya sebagai sumber kekayaan dan kesuburan serta penolakan duniawi dan kematian.",
        "en": "A Tantric hymn to Kali, the Karpuradistotra, describes her as the source of wealth and fertility but also of renunciation and death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c37",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "Another text describes her as sitting on a corpse in a cremation ground, surrounded by skulls, bones, and female jackals; it was believed that the Tantric practitioner (sadhaka) who fearlessly meditated on her in this guise would ultimately achieve salvation.",
      "locator": "Kali in the Hindu Texts",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebuah teks lain menggambarkannya duduk di atas mayat di tempat kremasi dikelilingi tengkorak, tulang, dan serigala betina; praktisi Tantrik yang bermeditasi tanpa takut kepadanya dalam wujud itu dipercaya akhirnya meraih keselamatan.",
        "en": "Another text describes her sitting on a corpse in a cremation ground, surrounded by skulls, bones, and female jackals; the Tantric practitioner who fearlessly meditated on her in this guise was believed to ultimately achieve salvation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c38",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "Because Kali is beyond both fear and ignorance, she can protect and enlighten those who invoke her. Thus she is often depicted with one of her four or more hands in the fear-removing gesture (abhaya mudra), and with another hand in the boon-granting gesture of compassion (varada mudra)",
      "locator": "Iconography",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena Kali melampaui rasa takut dan ketidaktahuan, ia dapat melindungi dan mencerahkan yang memanggilnya; ia sering digambarkan dengan satu tangan membuat gestur penghapus rasa takut (abhaya mudra) dan tangan lain gestur pemberi anugerah (varada mudra).",
        "en": "Because Kali is beyond fear and ignorance, she can protect and enlighten those who invoke her; she is often shown with one hand in the fear-removing gesture (abhaya mudra) and another in the boon-granting gesture (varada mudra)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c39",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "In her representation as Bhadrakali (auspicious Kali), her head is encompassed by the halo of flames and she wields the trident",
      "locator": "Iconography",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam wujud Bhadrakali (Kali yang membawa keberuntungan), kepalanya dikelilingi lingkaran api dan ia memegang trisula.",
        "en": "As Bhadrakali (auspicious Kali), her head is encircled by a halo of flames and she wields the trident."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c40",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "From the medieval period onwards, Kali devotees in Bengal have worshipped the goddess as Kali Ma-Kali, the benevolent mother.",
      "locator": "Kali worship",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejak Abad Pertengahan, para pemuja Kali di Bengal memuja sang dewi sebagai Kali Ma, ibu yang baik hati.",
        "en": "From the medieval period onwards Kali devotees in Bengal have worshipped the goddess as Kali Ma, the benevolent mother."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c41",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "Animal sacrifice is found alongside a less ritualistic form of worship introduced by the Bengali devotee Ramprasad Sen (1718-1775), who, in his poetry, approaches Kali as a child would its protective and nurturing mother.",
      "locator": "Kali worship",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pengorbanan hewan ada berdampingan dengan bentuk pemujaan yang kurang ritualistik yang diperkenalkan pemuja Bengal Ramprasad Sen (1718-1775), yang dalam puisinya mendekati Kali seperti anak kepada ibu yang melindungi dan mengasuh.",
        "en": "Animal sacrifice exists alongside a less ritualistic form of worship introduced by the Bengali devotee Ramprasad Sen (1718-1775), who in his poetry approaches Kali as a child would a protective and nurturing mother."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c42",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "Although predominant in Bengal, Kali is worshipped throughout India. There is the famous Kamakhya temple in Assam, as well as temples in Orissa, Rajasthan, and the Vindhya mountains of south-central India.",
      "locator": "Kali worship",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Walau dominan di Bengal, Kali dipuja di seluruh India; ada kuil Kamakhya yang terkenal di Assam, dan kuil-kuil di Orissa, Rajasthan, dan pegunungan Vindhya.",
        "en": "Although predominant in Bengal, Kali is worshipped throughout India; there is the famous Kamakhya temple in Assam, and temples in Orissa, Rajasthan, and the Vindhya mountains."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c43",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "where non-Hindus were introduced to Kali as a villainous goddess in several twentieth-century films, including Gunga Din (1939; loosely based on Rudyard Kipling's poem), the Beatles' Help! (1965), and Indiana Jones and the Temple of Doom (1984).",
      "locator": "A cult of Kali",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Barat, orang non-Hindu dikenalkan pada Kali sebagai dewi jahat dalam beberapa film abad ke-20, termasuk Gunga Din (1939), Help! karya The Beatles (1965), dan Indiana Jones and the Temple of Doom (1984).",
        "en": "In the West non-Hindus were introduced to Kali as a villainous goddess in several twentieth-century films, including Gunga Din (1939), the Beatles' Help! (1965), and Indiana Jones and the Temple of Doom (1984)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c44",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "for most modern worshippers Kali is not seen as being wrathful.",
      "locator": "A cult of Kali",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Encyclopedia.com, bagi sebagian besar pemuja masa kini Kali tidak dipandang sebagai murka.",
        "en": "According to Encyclopedia.com, for most modern worshippers Kali is not seen as being wrathful."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c45",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "The nationalist movement of the Bengalis in the late nineteenth and early twentieth centuries used the mother goddess in the form of Kali as a symbol of India.",
      "locator": "A cult of Kali",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gerakan nasionalis Bengal pada akhir abad ke-19 dan awal abad ke-20 memakai dewi ibu dalam wujud Kali sebagai simbol India.",
        "en": "The Bengali nationalist movement of the late nineteenth and early twentieth centuries used the mother goddess in the form of Kali as a symbol of India."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c46",
      "source_id": "kali-s4",
      "quote": "The goddess Pidari, who is most often associated with south India, is recognized as being an aspect of Kali, as are several other goddesses from Tamil Nadu.",
      "locator": "Kali",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dewi Pidari, yang paling sering dikaitkan dengan India selatan, dikenali sebagai salah satu aspek Kali, seperti beberapa dewi lain dari Tamil Nadu.",
        "en": "The goddess Pidari, most often associated with south India, is recognized as an aspect of Kali, as are several other goddesses from Tamil Nadu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c47",
      "source_id": "kali-s5",
      "quote": "also called Kalika, is a major goddess in Hinduism, primarily associated with time, death, and destruction.",
      "locator": "Lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kali, juga disebut Kalika, adalah dewi utama dalam agama Hindu yang terutama dikaitkan dengan waktu, kematian, dan kehancuran.",
        "en": "Kali, also called Kalika, is a major goddess in Hinduism, primarily associated with time, death, and destruction."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c48",
      "source_id": "kali-s5",
      "quote": "Kali is also connected with transcendental knowledge and is the first of the ten Mahavidyas, goddesses who provide liberating knowledge.",
      "locator": "Lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kali juga dikaitkan dengan pengetahuan transendental dan merupakan yang pertama dari sepuluh Mahavidya, dewi-dewi pemberi pengetahuan yang membebaskan.",
        "en": "Kali is also connected with transcendental knowledge and is the first of the ten Mahavidyas, goddesses who provide liberating knowledge."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c49",
      "source_id": "kali-s5",
      "quote": "Etymologically, Kali refers to one who governs time or is black.",
      "locator": "Lead",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Secara etimologis, Kali merujuk pada yang menguasai waktu atau yang hitam.",
        "en": "Etymologically, Kali refers to one who governs time or is black."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c50",
      "source_id": "kali-s5",
      "quote": "Kali is stated to protect and bestow liberation (moksha) to devotees who approach her as a child towards a mother.",
      "locator": "Lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kali dinyatakan melindungi dan menganugerahkan pembebasan (moksha) kepada pemuja yang mendekatinya seperti anak kepada ibu.",
        "en": "Kali is stated to protect and bestow liberation (moksha) on devotees who approach her as a child toward a mother."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c51",
      "source_id": "kali-s5",
      "quote": "In modern times, Kali has emerged as a significant symbol for women.",
      "locator": "Lead",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di masa modern, Kali telah menjadi simbol penting bagi perempuan.",
        "en": "In modern times Kali has emerged as a significant symbol for women."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kali-c52",
      "source_id": "kali-s5",
      "quote": "Kaushiki responds with such anger that it causes her face to turn dark, resulting in Kali appearing out of her forehead.",
      "locator": "Legends",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
